### Q206. MHT CET 2019 (02 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Two small drops of mercury each of radius ‘ <math><mi>R</mi></math> ’ coalesce to form a large single drop. The ratio of the total surface energies before and after the change is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><msup><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi></mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math><msqrt><mn>2</mn><mi></mi></msqrt><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math><msup><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>2</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><msup><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Surface enegy  <math><mo>∝</mo></math>  Surface Area 
<math><mo>∵</mo><mo> </mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>π</mi><msup><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>×</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>π</mi><mi></mi><msubsup><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msubsup></math>  
<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><mi>R</mi><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup></math>  
<math><mo>∴</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>π</mi><msup><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>×</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>π</mi><msup><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>  
<math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

### Q207. MHT CET 2019 (Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The excess of pressure, due to surface tension, on a spherical liquid drop of radius ‘R’ is proportional to

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><msup><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi>R</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><msup><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math><msup><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><msup><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

The excess of pressure due to surface tension on a spherically liquid drop is given by 
<math><mi>p</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math> ….(i) 
where, T = surface tension 
and R = radius of liquid drop. 
So, from Eq. (i), we get 
<math><mi>p</mi><mo>∝</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mi></mi><mi>o</mi><mi>r</mi><mi></mi><mi>p</mi><mo>∝</mo><msup><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

</details>

### Q208. MHT CET 2019 (Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Which one of the following statement is correct?

**Options:**

- **(A)** Surface energy is potential energy per unit length
- **(B)** Surface tension is work done per unit area
- **(C)** Surface tension is work done per unit length
- **(D)** Surface energy is work done per unit force

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Surface tension is work done per unit area**

**Detailed Solution:**

Surface tension is the force applied per unit length or work done (or energy) per unit area of a liquid surface. While surface energy is the amount of work done per unit area by the force.

</details>

### Q209. MHT CET 2019 (Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A molecule of water on the surface

**Options:**

- **(A)** Downward resultant unbalanced adhesive force
- **(B)** Upward resultant unbalanced cohesive force
- **(C)** Downward resultant unbalanced cohesive force
- **(D)** Upward resultant unbalanced adhesive force

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Downward resultant unbalanced cohesive force**

**Detailed Solution:**

A molecule on the surface experiences a net force (Cohesive force) in downward direction due to net number of molecules pulling it in the downward direction (molecule A as shown in figure underneath). 
In contrast, a molecule well inside is equally pulled in all directions by molecules around at (molecule B as shown in figure)

### Q210. MHT CET 2018

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

The radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi></math> of the soap bubble is doubled under isothermal condition. If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi></math> be the surface tension of the soap bubble, the work done in doing so is given by

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>32</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>T</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>24</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>T</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>T</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>T</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>24</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>T</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The surface energy of a bubble
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi></math> = Tension <math><mo>&#215;</mo></math> surface area
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>A</mi></math>
For soap bubble
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>T</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>A</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>T</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>
Here, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi></math> = radius of the bubble
If the radius is doubled then we have
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#8242;</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>T</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#8242;</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>T</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#8242;</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>32</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>T</mi></math></p><p>Work done = <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>&#39;</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>E</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>32</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>T</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>24</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>T</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q211. MHT CET 2018

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

In a capillary tube having area of cross-section <math> <mi>A</mi></math>, the water rises to a height <math><mi>h</mi></math>. If the cross-sectional area is reduced to <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>A</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>9</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>, the rise of water in the capillary tube is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mi>h</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>h</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>h</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math> <mi>h</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>h</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math> <mrow> <msub> <mi>A</mi> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo><mi>π</mi><msubsup> <mi>r</mi> <mn>1</mn> <mn>2</mn> </msubsup> </mrow> </math>
<math> <mrow> <msub> <mi>A</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo><mi>π</mi><msubsup> <mi>r</mi> <mn>2</mn> <mn>2</mn> </msubsup> </mrow> </math>
<math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>π</mi><msubsup> <mi>r</mi> <mn>1</mn> <mn>2</mn> </msubsup> </mrow> <mn>9</mn> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo><mi>π</mi><msubsup> <mi>r</mi> <mn>2</mn> <mn>2</mn> </msubsup> </mrow> </math>
<math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msubsup> <mi>r</mi> <mn>1</mn> <mn>2</mn> </msubsup> </mrow> <mrow> <msubsup> <mi>r</mi> <mn>2</mn> <mn>2</mn> </msubsup> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>⇒</mo><mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mi>r</mi> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mi>r</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow> </math>
<math> <mrow> <mi>r</mi><mi>h</mi><mo>=</mo></mrow> </math> constant
<math> <mrow> <msub> <mi>r</mi> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <msub> <mi>h</mi> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo><msub> <mi>r</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <msub> <mi>h</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math>
<math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mi>r</mi> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mi>r</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo><mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mi>h</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mi>h</mi> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
<math> <mrow> <mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mi>h</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mi>h</mi> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo><mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mi>h</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mi>h</mi> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
<math> <mrow> <msub> <mi>h</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><msub> <mi>h</mi> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>h</mi></mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q212. MHT CET 2017

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A big water drop is formed by the combination of <math><mi>n</mi></math> small water drops of equal radii. The ratio of the surface energy of <math><mi>n</mi></math> drops to the surface energy of the big drop is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><msup><mrow><mi>n</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi> </mi><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi>n</mi><mi> </mi><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math><msqrt><mi>n</mi></msqrt><mi> </mi><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math><mroot><mrow><mi>n</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mroot><mi> </mi><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mroot><mrow><mi>n</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mroot><mi> </mi><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Volume of big drop = <math><mi>n</mi></math> (Volume of small drop)
<math><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>π</mi><msup><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mo>.</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>π</mi><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math>
<math><msup><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math>
<math><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mi>n</mi></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup><mo>.</mo><mi>r</mi></math>
<math><msub><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mo>×</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>π</mi><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>×</mo><mi>T</mi></math>
<math><msub><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>π</mi><msup><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi>T</mi></math>
<math> <mtable columnalign='left'>  <mtr>   <mtd>    <mo>&#x2234;</mo><mo>&#x00A0;</mo><mfrac>     <mrow>      <msub>       <mi>E</mi>       <mn>2</mn>      </msub>      </mrow>     <mrow>      <msub>       <mi>E</mi>       <mn>1</mn>      </msub>      </mrow>    </mfrac>    <mo>=</mo><mfrac>     <mrow>      <mi>n</mi><msup>       <mi>r</mi>       <mn>2</mn>      </msup>      </mrow>     <mrow>      <msup>       <mi>R</mi>       <mn>2</mn>      </msup>      </mrow>    </mfrac>    <mo>=</mo><mfrac>     <mrow>      <mi>n</mi><msup>       <mi>r</mi>       <mn>2</mn>      </msup>      </mrow>     <mrow>      <msup>       <mrow>        <mrow><mo>(</mo>         <mrow>          <msup>           <mi>n</mi>           <mrow>            <mfrac>             <mn>1</mn>             <mn>3</mn>            </mfrac>            </mrow>          </msup>          <mo>.</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow>        <mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow>       <mn>2</mn>      </msup>      </mrow>    </mfrac>    <mo>=</mo><mfrac>     <mrow>      <mi>n</mi><msup>       <mi>r</mi>       <mn>2</mn>      </msup>      </mrow>     <mrow>      <msup>       <mi>n</mi>       <mrow>        <mfrac>         <mn>2</mn>         <mn>3</mn>        </mfrac>        </mrow>      </msup>      <mo>.</mo><msup>       <mi>r</mi>       <mn>2</mn>      </msup>      </mrow>    </mfrac>       </mtd>  </mtr>  <mtr>   <mtd>    <mo>&#x2234;</mo><mo>&#x00A0;</mo><mfrac>     <mrow>      <msub>       <mi>E</mi>       <mn>2</mn>      </msub>      </mrow>     <mrow>      <msub>       <mi>E</mi>       <mn>1</mn>      </msub>      </mrow>    </mfrac>    <mo>=</mo><msup>     <mi>n</mi>     <mrow>      <mfrac>       <mn>1</mn>       <mn>3</mn>      </mfrac>      </mrow>    </msup>    <mo>=</mo><mroot>     <mi>n</mi>     <mn>3</mn>    </mroot>    <mo>&#x00A0;</mo><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn>   </mtd>  </mtr> </mtable> </math>

</details>

### Q213. MHT CET 2017

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

When one end of the capillary is dipped in water, the height of the water column is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi></math>. The upward force of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>105</mn><mo> </mo><mi>dyne</mi></math> due to surface tension is balanced by the force due to the weight of the water column. The inner circumference of the capillary is

(Surface tension of water <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>)</mo></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>cm</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>cm</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>cm</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>cm</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>cm</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>105</mn><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>105</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>,<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>N</mo><msup><mo>m</mo><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><mi>r</mi><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>F</mi></math>
<math><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><mi>r</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>105</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>7</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mn>15</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>m</mo></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mn>1.5</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>m</mo></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mn>1.5</mn><mi> cm</mi></math>

</details>

### Q214. MHT CET 2016

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A liquid drop having surface energy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi></math> is spread into <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>512</mn></math> droplets of the same size. The final surface energy of the droplets is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>E</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mi>E</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mi>E</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>12</mn><mi>E</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mi>E</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

The surface area of the liquid drop is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>
Its surface energy is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi></math>
When the drop splits in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>512</mn></math> droplets, the surface area of each droplet is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>
<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8756;</mo> </mrow> </math> Total surface area <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">A</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>512</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>
The volume of the bigger drop is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> and the volume of small droplets is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>512</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>512</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>r</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>R</mi><mn>8</mn></mfrac></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><msub><mi>A</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>512</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>512</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mi>R</mi><mn>8</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><msub><mi>A</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>
Surface energy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>T</mi></math> (<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi></math> is surface tension and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> is area)
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></msub><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>A</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#8901;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mi>A</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#8901;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>8</mn><mo>&#8901;</mo><msub><mi>A</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><msub><mi>A</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msub><mi>E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>E</mi></math>

</details>

---

### Q215. MHT CET 2016

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

In a capillary tube of radius <math> <mi>R</mi></math>, a straight thin metal wire of radius <math> <mi>r</mi> </math> <math><mo>(</mo><mi>R</mi><mi> </mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mi> </mi><mi>r</mi><mo>)</mo></math> is inserted symmetrically and one end of the combination is dipped vertically in water such that the lower end of the combination is at same level. The rise of water in the capillary tube is



[<math> <mrow> <mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo></mrow> </math> surface tension of water, <math><mi>ρ</mi></math> = density of water, <math><mi>g</mi> <mo>=</mo> </math> gravitational acceleration]

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>R</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>ρ</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>2T</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>R</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>ρ</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>R</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>ρ</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>R</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>ρ</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>R</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>ρ</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi>h</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>T</mi><mi>c</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>θ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>ρ</mi><mi>g</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>R</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math> 
For  <math><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi> </mi><mi>θ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi></mrow></mrow></math>   <math><mi>h</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>ρ</mi><mi>g</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>R</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

### Q216. MHT CET 2011

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The radius $R$ of the soap bubble is doubled under isothermal condition. If $T$ be the surface tension of soap bubble. The work done in doing so it given by

**Options:**

- **(A)** $32 \pi R{ }^{2} T$
- **(B)** $24 \pi R^{2} T$
- **(C)** $8 \pi R^{2} T$
- **(D)** $4 \pi R^{2} T$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $32 \pi R{ }^{2} T$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Surface energy of a bubble
$$
\begin{array}{l}
E=\text { Tension } \times \text { surface area } \\
E=T \times A
\end{array}
$$
For soap bubble
$$
\begin{array}{l}
E=2 T \times A \\
E=2 T \times 4 \pi R^{2}
\end{array}
$$
Here, $\quad R=$ radius of bubble
If radius is doubled then we have
$$
\begin{array}{l}
E^{\prime}=2 T \times 4 \pi R^{2} \\
E^{\prime}=2 T \times 4 \pi(2 R)^{2} \\
E^{\prime}=32 \pi R^{2} T
\end{array}
$$

</details>

### Q217. MHT CET 2010

- **(C)** maximum kinetic energy

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) maximum potential energy**

**Detailed Solution:**

On the surface of the liquid in equilibrium molecules of the liquid possess maximum potential energy.

### Q218. MHT CET 2009

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

The change in energy when a big dropis split in sma $\| n$ droplets is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4 R^{2}\left(n^{2 / 3}-1\right) I$
- **(B)** $4 \pi R^{2}\left(n^{1 / 3}-1\right) T$
- **(C)** $4 \pi R^{2}\left(n^{-1 / 3}-1\right) T$
- **(D)** $4 \pi R^{2}\left[n^{-2 / 3}-1\right] T$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $4 \pi R^{2}\left(n^{1 / 3}-1\right) T$**

**Detailed Solution:**

When a drop of radius $R$ split into $n$ smaller drops (each of radius $r$ ), then surface area of liquid increases. Hence, the work is to be done against surface tension. Since, the volume of liquid remains constant
$$
\begin{array}{l}
\therefore \quad \frac{4}{3} \pi R^{3}=n \frac{4}{3} \pi r^{3} \quad \therefore R^{3}=n r^{3} \\
\text { Work done }=T \times \Delta A \\
=T\left[n 4 \pi r^{2}-4 \pi R^{2}\right] \\
=4 \pi R^{2} T\left(n^{1 / 3}-1\right)
\end{array}
$$

</details>

### Q219. MHT CET 2008

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The potential energy of a molecule on the surface of a liquid compared to one inside the liquid is

**Options:**

- **(A)** zero
- **(B)** lesser
- **(C)** equal
- **(D)** greater

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) greater**

**Detailed Solution:**

When the surface area of a liquid is increased, molecules from the interior of the liquid rise to the surface. As these molecules reach the surface work is done against the cohesive force. This work is stored in the molecules in the form of potential energy. Thus, the potential energy of the molecules lying in the surface is greater than that of the molecules in the interior of the liquid.

</details>

---

### Q220. MHT CET 2008

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A frame made of metallic wire enclosing a surface area $A$ is covered with a soap film. If the area of the frame of metallic wire is reduced by $50 \%$, the energy of the soap film will be changed by

**Options:**

- **(A)** $100 \%$
- **(B)** $75 \%$
- **(C)** $50 \%$
- **(D)** $25 \%$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $50 \%$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Surface energy $=$ surface tension $\times$ surface area
$$
E=T \times 2 A
$$
New surface energy,
$$
E_{1}=T \times 2\left(\frac{A}{2}\right)=T \times A
$$
$\%$ decrease in surface energy
$$
\begin{array}{l}
=\frac{E-E_{1}}{E} \times 100 \\
=\frac{2 T A-T A}{2 T A} \times 100=50 \%
\end{array}
$$

</details>

### Q221. MHT CET 2007

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

According to Hooke's law of elasticity, if stress is increased, then the ratio of stress to strain

**Options:**

- **(A)** becomes zero
- **(B)** remains constant
- **(C)** decreases
- **(D)** increases

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) remains constant**

**Detailed Solution:**

Hooke in 1679 , showed experimentally that if strain is small, then the stress is proportional to strain. The ratio of stress to strain is constant for material of the given body and is called modulus of elasticity $E$. Thus, $E=\frac{\text { stress }}{\text { strain }}=$ constant
Hence, if stress is increased, then the ratio of stress to strain remains constant.

</details>
